## Normalisation register — all eighteen, with their lessons

| ID | ❌ Source statement | ✅ Correction | Lesson |
|---|---|---|---|
| **N1** | *"GSSP is the KNI-51 stalagmite in Mawmluh Cave"* | **KM-A at 7.45 mm**; **KNI-51 is a cave in Western Australia**, auxiliary stratotype | **L14** |
| **N2** | *"Karst district of Yugoslavia"* / *"Krs Plateau of Yugoslavia"* | **Kras plateau, Slovenia**, extending into **Italy**; Postojna in Slovenia; **Dinaric** belt | L1, L9 |
| **N3** | *"The climate should be humid-temperate. Karst topography does not develop in desert areas"* | Climate governs **rate and style**, not possibility; tropical karst is the most vigorous; arid karst is **relict** | L2, **L8**, L11 |
| **N4** | Limestone and chalk are *"of organic origin… corals and shells"* | Add **chemical/biochemical precipitation**, **microfossil ooze**, **ooids**; chalk is **coccolith ooze** | L1 |
| **N5** | Three different definitions of a doline across three sources | **Doline and sinkhole overlap** = the basic closed depression; **uvala = coalesced dolines** | **L6** |
| **N6** | Meghalaya plateau *"largely formed by Archaean (Dharwarian) quartzites, shales and schists"* | True of the **basement**; the caves are in the **Palaeogene Sylhet Limestone, Shella Fm, Jaintia Gp, Eocene** | **L11** |
| **N7** | *"When this lime-charged water evaporates"*; *"water evaporates as it splashes"* | **CO2 degassing is primary**; evaporation secondary and local | **L7**, L12 |
| **N8** | World list omits tropical Asia and India | Add **South China** and **mainland SE Asia**; **declare the addition** | L9 |
| **N9** | Condition 1 *"thickly bedded"* vs condition 2 *"preferably thinly bedded"* | **Matrix** vs **discontinuities**; **drop the bedding-thickness clause** | L3 |
| **N10** | *"Ponores — a vertical pipe-like passage"* | A **ponor is the point of stream sinking**; the book's usage is narrow and structural | L5 |
| **N11** | A speleothem is *"a column of pure limestone"* | A **calcite precipitate**, not a rock | L7, L12 |
| **N12** | Terra rossa's colour comes from *"the dissolution of clay to form iron-oxide"* | **Residual accumulation of insoluble clay and iron oxides** left when carbonate dissolves | L6 |
| **N13** | *"Mount Everest is composed of Upper Carboniferous limestone and dolomite"* | The summit carbonate is **Ordovician** | L10 |
| **N14** | *"Shillong (1,961 m) is the highest point of the plateau"* | **1,961 m is Shillong Peak**, not the town | L11 |
| **N15** | Limestone resources *"84,935 million tonnes"* | **184,935 Mt**; components sum to it and the printed percentages confirm it | L10 |
| **N16** | Four incompatible limestone rankings | They measure **resources / cement-grade reserves / production 2011-12 / production 2007-08**. **Teach the distinction, not a league table** | L10 |
| **N17** | Mawmluh *"recognised by UNESCO"* | The First 100 is an **IUGS** designation; **GSI** declares geo-heritage sites; a **Tentative List entry is not an inscription** | **L15** |
| **N18** | Mawsynram rainfall *"about 11,875 m"*; elsewhere *"1080 cm"* | **Unit error** (millimetres) and an **internal contradiction**; IMD-attributed normals are press-sourced; **prefer the qualitative form** | L11 |

## Scope, boundary and typing

- A **Mains-focused** edition of Geography Topic 08, integrating the world/general Basic owner with its India-applied Advanced companion in **one file, one lesson sequence, sixteen lessons**. ❗ **Arc A (L1–L9) is the world process; Arc B (L10–L15) is the India application; L16 is the capstone.** ❗ Arc B is **not** an appendix: Arc A exists to make Arc B answerable and Arc B exists to make Arc A examinable, and **every Arc-B lesson back-references an Arc-A mechanism by lesson number**.
- **Ownership stated precisely.** ⚠️ This topic owns **the solution process, the landforms it makes above and below ground, the aquifer behaviour that follows from conduit flow, the natural caves of India and their host rock, the speleothem proxy method, and the formal definition of the Meghalayan boundary.** ❗ It does **not** own groundwater as a resource, river-cut landforms, sea caves, Indian stratigraphy as a system, rock-cut monuments, civilisational history, cave-biodiversity or mining policy, cement-industry economics, or tourism economics.
- ❗ **A syllabus-mapping gap is reported, not amended.** The repository's official syllabus-mapping file cites **neither `basic/08` nor `advanced/08` under any clause**. ⚠️ This edition therefore claims the **demand families it substantively serves** and cites the subject master framework's landform-processes line — which names karst geomorphology explicitly — as its in-repository mapping authority. ❗ **Amending the mapping file is a separate, separately-authorised task and was not performed.**
- **Typing convention.** ✅ marks a statement carried in a named source. ⚠️ marks analytical inference, synthesis, evaluation or a flagged limitation. 📰 marks a dated current or institutional item with its source class. 🔁 marks an ownership routing or cross-lesson reference. ❗ marks a hard prohibition or a checkable discipline. ❌ marks an error being corrected. ❓ marks a question this edition declines to settle. 🇮🇳 marks an India-specific application. 🔑 marks a memory aid.

## Page conventions, recorded so no citation here is mistaken for another edition's pagination

- **Majid Husain, *Indian and World Geography*** uses **chapter-relative folios** of the form `<chapter>.<page>`. It is the **only text-searchable local volume**, and its citations take the form **printed p.X.YZ (local scan PDF p.N)**.
- **G.C. Leong, *Certificate Physical and Human Geography***: ❗ **no printed folio is legible on any page of the limestone chapter.** Footer crops at 220 dots per inch show the page number is **cropped out of the underlying scan**; header crops at 260 dots per inch show only a watermark. ❗ **Chapter 8 is therefore cited by local scan PDF page only, and no printed folio is stated for any of its pages anywhere in this file.**
- **D.R. Khullar, *India: A Comprehensive Geography***, carries **two printed folios per scan page, stacked and rotated 90 degrees**, with an offset that **drifts across the book**. ❗ Folios are **read off each render and never computed**, and citations give both the printed folio and the local scan PDF page.
- **Majid Husain, *Geography of India***, is image-only; its entire text layer is a watermark and a copyright stamp. ❗ Classified **LOW-yield** after its contents pages were read in full, which established that it contains **no karst, limestone, cave or speleothem section anywhere**. ❗ **No claim in this file is attributed to it beyond the Cuddapah and Vindhyan context at printed 1.6–1.8, and even there the other volume is preferred.**

| **CA1** | Government of Meghalaya, Directorate of Mineral Resources — *Technical Data on the Various Limestone Deposits of Meghalaya* | ✅ **Official — state government** | Dossier undated on its face; cites GSI and Cement Corporation investigations of **1962, 1966-67 and 1977** | The Sylhet/Shella stratigraphy (Lakadong, Umlatdoh, Prang; Shella Formation, Jaintia Group, Eocene; Kopili above; coal-bearing Sylhet Sandstone below); purity ranges; borehole thicknesses; deposit areas; the Dawki-parallel fault at Komorrah; and the named operators — *"mined by M/S Mawmluh Cherra Cement Company"*, *"Lumshnong is presently mined by three Cement plants"*, *"exported by Lafarge Umiam Pvt. Ltd for their plant at Bangladesh"* |
| **CA2** | UNESCO World Heritage Centre — tentative-list entry 6801, **Kanger Valley National Park**, submitted by the Permanent Delegation of India | ✅ **Official — UNESCO WHC** | **11 February 2025**; criteria (vii), (viii), (x) | The only official Indian statement of a karst process: *"unique karst landform"*; *"complete lithological succession of the Indravati Group"*; caves formed because of *"faults, folds, joints, rivers"*; *"more than 20 limestone caves"*; *"caves decorated with beautiful speleothems, sinkholes, and solution valleys"*; the speleothem vocabulary including helictites, flowstone and rimstone; Kotamsar, Dandak, Kailash and Aranyak; **five cave-restricted spider species**; 200 sq km, Bastar, established 1982; the hedged Pre-Pliocene evolution statement |
| **CA3** | International Union of Geological Sciences — *"The First 100 IUGS Geological Heritage Sites"* | ✅ **Official — IUGS** | **25–28 October 2022**, Zumaia, Basque Coast UNESCO Global Geopark, Spain | The designation, its date and venue; *"more than 200 specialists from almost 40 nations and ten international organisations"*; **Mawmluh Cave is India's entry**. ❗ The per-site page returned an access error and **no site citation text is quoted** |
| **CA4** | Press Information Bureau / Ministry of Mines — *"Geo-Heritage Sites"* | ✅ **Official — PIB** | **9 March 2016** | *"Geological Survey of India (GSI) declares geo-heritage sites / national geological monuments for protection and maintenance… GSI or the respective State governments take necessary measures to protect these sites"*; *"**There is no proposal pending with UNESCO** for declaration of geo-heritage sites"*; the state-wise annexure read in full, in which **no cave appears** and the only carbonate entry is the **Buxa Formation stromatolitic dolomite/limestone at Mamley, near Namchi**, Sikkim |
| **CA5** | International Commission on Stratigraphy — the official **GSSP procedure** page | ✅ **Official — ICS/IUGS** | Undated standing procedure; **retrieved 2026-10-04** | The correlation criterion; the **>60 per cent** subcommission supermajority, the ICS vote and **IUGS Executive Committee** ratification; *"at the GSSP stratotype locality, a physical marker should be placed"*; the **ten-year** moratorium on change proposals |
| **CA6** | Government of Meghalaya Tourism — **Krem Puri** | ✅ **Official — state government** | Discovery **2016**; page **retrieved 2026-10-04** | *"Krem Puri—meaning 'fairy cave' in Khasi—is the world's longest sandstone cave… near Mawsynram… Spanning over 24 kilometres… Discovered in 2016… with fossils of marine reptiles like mosasaurs embedded in its sandstone."* ❗ Length cited as survey-dependent; the superlative reported as the state's own characterisation |
| **CA7** | Belum Caves declared a **GSI geo-heritage site** | ⚠️ **Press, reporting a GSI announcement** | **June 2025** | Kolimigundla mandal, **Nandyal** district, Andhra Pradesh; reported as the state's seventh such site. ❗ **The GSI notification was not retrieved; no notification number is cited** |
| **CA8** | *"Caving in the Abode of the Clouds"* expedition | ⚠️ **Press** | **2–28 February**; ❗ **the reported year is internally inconsistent in the source and is not resolved here** | Supported by the Meghalaya Adventurers Association and sponsored by the state government; **24 cavers** from seven countries; **22,663 m** of new passage; **27 caves explored, 18 newly identified**; **Wangkung Khlur extended to 13,618 m**; cumulative mapped length reported as nearing **574 km**. ❗ Used to demonstrate the volatility rule, never as a settled ranking |

No outcome exists and none is implied** |

⚠️ **Nine of the sixteen lessons — L1, L2, L3, L4, L5, L6, L9, L10 and L12 — declare in their pre-teach checklist that no official source was obtained for that lesson's content.** ❗ That is a true statement about world-process mechanism and book-sourced Indian stratigraphy, and ❗ **no anchor has been borrowed from a neighbouring lesson or invented to disguise it.** ❗ **Three lessons — L12, L13 and L14 — further declare that they have no book basis at all**, because no local volume contains palaeoclimate, chronostratigraphic or Meghalayan-Age content of any kind.

| The IUGS per-site page for Mawmluh Cave | ❗ **Access error.** ❗ Only the **First 100 announcement** is cited; **no site citation text is quoted** |
| The state tourism caving index and the page for the cave most often cited as India's longest | ❗ The index returned an access error; the cave page returned **generic travel advice only**. ❗ **No official cave-length statement was obtainable, which is why the no-numbers rule is absolute here** |
| A national aquifer-mapping document from the responsible central water authority | ❗ **Retrieved and read, but effectively empty for this topic:** across approximately fifty scanned pages it yields exactly **one** incidental mention of limestone among *"soft rock aquifers"* and **no statement on karst-aquifer behaviour or vulnerability**. ❗ **Nothing is attributed to it** |
| The IMD rainfall-normals table | ❗ **Not fetched.** Figures are **press-attributed to IMD** and are cited with that attribution and their normal periods, or replaced by the qualitative form |
